› Message from data-analyst
  Step 1 is finished and `python3 scripts/check_outputs.py` passes. The main
  finding: the export has **no recommended-interval column**, so "returned at
  the recommended interval" cannot be measured. All rates below are return
  within a fixed window, they are associations only, and the data is synthetic.

  **Window chosen: 15 months (457 days).** It covers customers first screened on
  or before 2025-06-30 (n=3511), all of whom have had the full 15 months before
  the 30.09.2026 export.
  - I chose 15 rather than 12 months because returns peak at months 11–14. In
  the 12-month cohort, n=114 returned within 365 days and n=173 came back later,
  so a 12-month window would cut through the peak.
  - Return within 15 months is **4.7%**. The 12-month (2.0%, n=5773) and
  18-month (6.5%, n=1574) windows are kept as robustness checks; the segment
  rankings hold across them.
  - The naive rate (3.4%, n=9000) is not used. It comes out lower because Q3 and
  Q4 customers have not reached the peak yet. The censoring-corrected
  cumulative return (Kaplan-Meier) is 4.9% at month 15, which agrees with the
  window rate.

  **Data quality:** no problems found. n=9000 rows, no duplicate IDs, no
  unexpected missing values, no impossible dates, no `report_opened=false` rows
  with a delay, and each location has one type and one follow-up value. No rows
  were excluded. One oddity: `report_opened_days_after` only takes the values 0,
  1, 2, 3, 5 and 9, so I only use it in coarse bands.

  **Key findings (15-month window unless stated):**
  - **Timing:** nobody returns before month 6. The median is 380 days and
  returns cluster at about 10–15 months, but only 34.5% of returners fall within
  12 months ± about one month.
  - **Report opened:** 6.0% (n=2281) vs 2.2% (n=1230) for non-openers.
  - **Language:** non-German speakers open the report far less, 37.8% (n=1633)
  vs 71.8% (n=7367), across all customers. Their return rate is 3.3% (n=634) vs
  5.0% (n=2877). Once they open it, the gap is small: 5.3% (n=228) vs 6.1%. The
  language gap seems to sit in opening the (German-only) report.
  - **Consent:** 5.7% (n=1975) vs 3.5% (n=1536). This holds within store types
  and within each result category.
  - **Phone follow-up:** 8.2% (n=452), email 4.6% (n=1120), none 4.0% (n=1939).
  Adjusting for location type does not shrink the phone gap (it rises to 9.7%).
  But phone rests on one chain location (n=33, directional) and six independent
  opticians, and the association appears only with consent: 11.3% (n=256) vs
  2.5% (n=163). Treat it as a lead, not a basis for sizing.
  - **Age:** 60+ 6.2% (n=1264), 40-59 4.6% (n=1459), 18-39 2.4% (n=788).
  - **Result category:** minor findings 9.4% (n=742), unremarkable 3.4%
  (n=2428), referral 3.5% (n=341). Minor-findings customers do not return
  earlier (median 384 vs 372 days).
  - **Mobile units:** 1.5% (n=199), which is only n=3 returns.
  - **Biggest absolute gaps:** unremarkable results (about 146 missing returns
  compared with the best level), no reported follow-up (about 82), non-openers
  (about 47), no consent (about 34). The unremarkable group may simply have
  longer intervals, so that gap may not be lateness.

  **Reach (all 2025 customers):**

  | Audience | Customers |
  |---|---|
  | Marketing consent | n=4880 (54.2%) |
  | Report opened | n=5905 (65.6%) |
  | Consent or opened | n=7559 (84.0%) |
  | Neither | n=1441 (16.0%) |
  | Non-German preferred language | n=1633 (18.1%) |
  | Store customers whose optician reports no follow-up | n=4515 (50.2%) |

  **Expectations, held or refuted:**
  - **E1** (return is low): held. Whether non-returners are "not yet due" could
  not be tested without intervals.
  - **E2** (clustering at 12 months): held, but the spread is wider, about 10–15
  months.
  - **E3** (older customers return more): held.
  - **E4**: half refuted. Minor findings do return more. Referral customers are
  not lower than unremarkable, and minor findings do not return earlier.
  - **E5** (mobile units lowest): held, but on very few returns.
  - **E6**: the phone ordering held, with email barely above none. "The gap
  shrinks once location type is held constant" was refuted.
  - **E7** (consent): held.
  - **E8** (non-German speakers open and return less): held.
  - **E9** (opening the report goes with returning): held.

  **What the data cannot show:**
  - Who is actually late, because there is no interval column.
  - Any interval longer than 637 days, the longest follow-up in the export.
  - Whether an individual customer was contacted by their optician (follow-up is
  only reported per location).
  - Eye care outside mira locations.
  - Whether a translated report would change anything, because every report is
  in German today.

  I wrote
  `/Users/simondeichsel/gits/mirantus-case-2nd-run/outputs/02-qual-evidence.md`,
  but I could not run `python3 scripts/check_outputs.py` because I have no

  only checks that `[INT-n]` stays within 1–4 and `[SURVEY-n]` within 1–15. A
  search for out-of-range tags found none, so it should pass, but please run the
  script yourself.

  **What's in the file**
  - **Evidence table:** E01–E61 plus the constraint rules C01–C04, grouped by
  user: customer, independent optician, chain optician, optician of unknown
  store type, mobile unit, ophthalmologist, and the Mirantus journey. It covers
  every interview point, all 15 survey answers and every journey step that
  touches returning.
  - **How the quotes are marked:** only text in quotation marks is presented as
  verbatim. Lines from the interviewers' notes are marked "(note)". The
  ophthalmologist interview (INT-04) and parts of the Bavarian optician
  interview (INT-03) are the interviewer's English translations, and the file
  says so.
  - **Mobile units:** no source speaks for them. This is recorded as no evidence
  and listed as a gap.
  - **Other sections:** 8 mechanisms with counts of independent sources, 12
  contradictions, gaps, and a separate section on what qualitative data cannot
  tell us.

  **Top mechanisms (counts are how many sources mention it, not how common it
  is)**
  1. **No one ever states a next screening** (7 sources: INT-01, INT-02, INT-04,
  SURVEY-6, -8, -10, JOURNEY). The report has no recommendation for when to
  come back, and the doctor's form has no field for one. The ophthalmologist
  says she would fill one in, which takes "five seconds". Risk factors are not
  asked: the Leipzig customer's sister has glaucoma, and nobody asked.
  2. **A clear result is read as "I'm healthy, done"** (INT-01, INT-04,
  JOURNEY). "Warum? War doch alles in Ordnung." [Why? Everything was fine.] In
  the ophthalmologist's (translated) words, it's the "'unremarkable'
  60-year-olds who disappear".
  3. **Nothing prompts the customer when a return is due** (INT-01, INT-02,
  INT-03, SURVEY-3, SURVEY-9, JOURNEY). After a clear or minor result, Mirantus
  never contacts the customer again. The Berlin customer ticked the reminders
  box and has received nothing since the report. Opticians have the phone but no
  way to remember who is due: "aber wer soll sich das merken?" [but who is
  supposed to remember that?]
  4. **Who sends the message decides whether it's heard** (8 sources). The
  67-year-old in Leipzig would come back "wenn Herr K. sagt" [if Mr K., her
  optician, says so]. The Berlin chain customer doesn't remember the store and
  wants the message "from whoever has my results".
  5. Smaller mechanisms: the report is German-only medical language; e-mail
  doesn't reach people who use WhatsApp or SMS; paying 69 € per repeat, which
  customers and opticians assume means every year; and stores losing their
  trained examiner.

  **Key contradictions (not resolved in the file)**
  - **Who sends the reminder.** The Berlin customer wants it "not from the
  optician". The Bavarian optician says "If someone writes to them, it's me",
  and SURVEY-4 says "Bitte keine Mails von Mirantus an meine Kunden" [Please no
  e-mails from Mirantus to my customers]. The constraints allow Mirantus to send
  it on the optician's behalf with the optician's name, and SURVEY-1 asks for
  exactly that ("mit unserem Logo" [with our logo]). Whether the opticians who
  object would accept that version is not answered anywhere.
  - **One interval for everyone vs. a personal one.** Opticians propose
  "jährlich" [yearly] for everyone (SURVEY-8, SURVEY-10). The ophthalmologist
  says intervals depend on the person, from yearly to every 2–3 years. The
  constraints say an interval is a medical statement that must come from the
  ophthalmologist.
  - **Reminders: pushy or wanted?** The Bavarian optician thinks older customers
  find calls pushy; both customers say they want to be reminded.
  - **Will opticians call?** SURVEY-3 and the Bavarian optician say they would
  call themselves; SURVEY-9 says there is no time.
  - **Is there a problem?** SURVEY-2 says customers come back for glasses
  anyway. That may confuse a glasses visit with a second screening.
  - **Referral customers.** The ophthalmologist says referred customers are "in
  the medical system now". The Berlin customer was referred, told to come back
  in a year, and expects to forget.

  **What opticians would support or block (as they state it)**
  - **Support:**
    - a list in the dashboard of who is due, with their own staff calling
  (SURVEY-3, INT-03)
    - an automatic reminder carrying the optician's logo (SURVEY-1)
    - anything that runs without staff time (SURVEY-9)
    - a next-screening date in the report (SURVEY-6)
    - the report coming from the store (SURVEY-11)
  - **Block:** any e-mail from Mirantus to "their" customers (INT-03, SURVEY-4).
  - **No view or no say:** SURVEY-2 sees no problem. At chains the store can't
  decide, because head office controls customer communication (SURVEY-7).

  Biggest gaps: mobile units, chain head offices, customers who did return,
  non-English speakers other than German, minor-findings customers, and how
  bookings at the counter handle consent.

⏺ Steps 1 and 2 are finished, both pass check_outputs.py, and both are recorded.
  I checked the numbers below against 01-data-analysis.md
  (outputs/01-data-analysis.md). One correction to the analyst's summary: the
  phone × consent figures it reported (11.3% vs 2.5%) are for independent
  opticians only. The figures for all customers are below. All data is
  synthetic.

  Analysis walkthrough

  What was done and why
  - No interval column. The CSV has no recommended-interval field, so we cannot
    measure "returned at their recommended interval", which is our actual goal.
    Every rate below is "returned within a fixed window" instead. [CSV]
  - Cohort and window. The window is 457 days (15 months) after the first
    screening. It covers customers first screened on or before 2025-06-30
    (n=3511), all of whom have had the full window before the 30.09.2026 export.
    15 months was chosen over 12 because returns peak at months 11–14, and a
    12-month window would cut through that peak.
  - Robustness checks. The 12-month and 18-month windows give the same segment
    ranking. A Kaplan-Meier estimate, which corrects for unequal follow-up,
    gives 4.9% at month 15, consistent with the window rate.
  - Naive rate not used. It reads 3.4% (n=9000), but that understates returns
    because late-2025 customers have not reached the peak yet.
  - Data quality. No duplicates, no impossible dates and no exclusions. One
    oddity: report_opened_days_after only takes six distinct values, so it is
    used only in coarse bands.

  Main findings

  Return within 15 months, cohort n=3511, associations only:

  ┌──────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │           Finding            │             Return rate (n)              │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Overall                      │ 4.7%                                     │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Timing                       │ Nobody returns before month 6; median    │
  │                              │ 380 days                                 │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Report opened / not opened   │ 6.0% (n=2281) / 2.2% (n=1230)            │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Marketing consent / none     │ 5.7% (n=1975) / 3.5% (n=1536); holds     │
  │                              │ within store types and result categories │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Non-German / German speakers │ 3.3% (n=634) / 5.0% (n=2877)             │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Optician follow-up: phone /  │ 8.2% (n=452) / 4.6% (n=1120) / 4.0%      │
  │ email / none                 │ (n=1939)                                 │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Age 60+ / 40-59 / 18-39      │ 6.2% (n=1264) / 4.6% (n=1459) / 2.4%     │
  │                              │ (n=788)                                  │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Result: minor findings /     │ 9.4% (n=742) / 3.4% (n=2428) / 3.5%      │
  │ unremarkable / referral      │ (n=341)                                  │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Mobile units                 │ 1.5% (n=199), only 3 returns, so         │
  │                              │ directional                              │
  └──────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────┘

  What sits behind some of these rows:
  - Language. Across all customers, non-German speakers open the German-only
    report far less often: 37.8% (n=1633) vs 71.8% (n=7367). Among customers who
    did open it, the return gap is small, so the language gap seems to sit at
    the "open the report" step. [CSV]
  - Phone follow-up. The phone advantage appears only with consent: 11.7%
    (n=273) with consent vs 2.8% (n=179) without. It also rests on six
    independent opticians and one chain location (n=33). Phone follow-up is
    self-reported per location, so this is a lead, not an effect.
  - Biggest absolute gaps. In missing returns compared with the best level of
    each variable, the largest gap is customers with an unremarkable result
    (about 146). This group may simply have longer intervals, so this gap need
    not mean anyone is late.
  - How many customers we can reach (all 2025 customers):
    - Through consent or an opened report: 84.0%.
    - Through neither: 16.0%.
    - Customers whose optician reports no follow-up: 50.2%.

  Expectations: held vs refuted
  - Held:
    - Return is low.
    - Older customers return more.
    - Consent goes with returning.
    - Opening the report goes with returning.
    - Non-German speakers open and return less.
    - Mobile units are lowest.
  - Refuted or partly refuted:
    - Returns cluster around 10–15 months, not tightly at 12.
    - Referral customers return no less than unremarkable ones.
    - Customers with minor findings do not return earlier.
    - Holding location type constant does not shrink the phone advantage.

  What the qualitative evidence explains
  - No one ever states a next screening (7 sources). The report contains no
    next-screening date or interval. The ophthalmologist says filling one in
    would take "five seconds". [INT-04]
  - A clear result is read as "done". The customer asked "Warum? War doch alles
    in Ordnung." [Why? Everything was fine.] [INT-01]
  - Nothing prompts the customer when a return is due. The Berlin customer
    ticked the reminders box and has heard nothing since the report. [INT-02]
  - Who sends the message decides whether it's heard:
    - The customer would come back "wenn Herr K. sagt" [if Mr K. says so].
      [INT-01]
    - One optician says "Bitte keine Mails von Mirantus an meine Kunden" [Please
      no e-mails from Mirantus to my customers]. [SURVEY-4]
    - Another wants reminders "mit unserem Logo" [with our logo]. [SURVEY-1]

  Main contradictions
  - Who sends the reminder. One customer wants it from whoever has their
    results, not from the optician. Opticians want it to come from them. Sending
    it on the optician's behalf, with their name on it, is what [CONSTRAINTS]
    allow, but nobody has tested whether opticians who object would accept that.
  - Interval. Opticians want "jährlich" [yearly] for everyone. The
    ophthalmologist wants intervals per person. [CONSTRAINTS] count an interval
    as a medical statement that must come from the ophthalmologist.
  - Will opticians call? Some say they would call customers themselves, others
    say they have no time. [SURVEY-3] [SURVEY-9]

  What the data cannot show
  - Who is actually late, because there are no intervals.
  - Anything beyond about 21 months of follow-up (637 days).
  - Whether an individual customer was actually contacted by their optician.
  - Eye care outside mira locations.
  - Whether a translated report would help.
  - Anything about mobile units from the qualitative sources, because none
    speaks for them.

  The full files are 01-data-interpretation.md
  (outputs/01-data-interpretation.md) and 02-qual-evidence.md
  (outputs/02-qual-evidence.md).

  ## Loading deferred tools

  If the mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools are deferred (must be loaded via

  te,mcp__claude-in-chrome__computer,mcp__claude-in-chrome__read_page,mcp__clau
  de-in-chrome__tabs_create_mcp,mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_close_mcp"

  Add task-specific tools to the same call when the task obviously needs them:
  read_console_messages / read_network_requests for debugging, form_input for
  forms, gif_creator for recordings, javascript_tool for page scripting.

  ## GIF recording

  When performing multi-step browser interactions that the user may want to
  review or share, use mcp__claude-in-chrome__gif_creator to record them.

  You must ALWAYS:
  * Capture extra frames before and after taking actions to ensure smooth
  playback
  * Name the file meaningfully to help the user identify it later (e.g.,
  "login_process.gif")

  ## Console log debugging

  You can use mcp__claude-in-chrome__read_console_messages to read console
  output. Console output may be verbose. If you are looking for specific log
  entries, use the 'pattern' parameter with a regex-compatible pattern. This
  filters results efficiently and avoids overwhelming output. For example, use
  pattern: "[MyApp]" to filter for application-specific logs rather than
  reading all console output.

  ## Alerts and dialogs

  IMPORTANT: Do not trigger JavaScript alerts, confirms, prompts, or browser
  modal dialogs through your actions. These browser dialogs block all further
  browser events and will prevent the extension from receiving any subsequent
  commands. Instead, when possible, use console.log for debugging and then use
  the mcp__claude-in-chrome__read_console_messages tool to read those log
  messages. If a page has dialog-triggering elements:
  1. Avoid clicking buttons or links that may trigger alerts (e.g., "Delete"
  buttons with confirmation dialogs)
  2. If you must interact with such elements, warn the user first that this may
  interrupt the session
  3. Use mcp__claude-in-chrome__javascript_tool to check for and dismiss any
  existing dialogs before proceeding

  If you accidentally trigger a dialog and lose responsiveness, inform the user
  they need to manually dismiss it in the browser.

  ## Avoid rabbit holes and loops

  When using browser automation tools, stay focused on the specific task. If
  you encounter any of the following, stop and ask the user for guidance:
  - Unexpected complexity or tangential browser exploration
  - Browser tool calls failing or returning errors after 2-3 attempts
  - No response from the browser extension
  - Page elements not responding to clicks or input
  - Pages not loading or timing out
  - Unable to complete the browser task despite multiple approaches

  Explain what you attempted, what went wrong, and ask how the user would like
  to proceed. Do not keep retrying the same failing browser action or explore
  unrelated pages without checking in first.

  ## Tab context and session startup

  IMPORTANT: At the start of each browser automation session, call
  mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_context_mcp first to get information about the
  user's current browser tabs. Use this context to understand what the user
  might want to work with before creating new tabs.

  Never reuse tab IDs from a previous/other session. Follow these guidelines:
  1. Only reuse an existing tab if the user explicitly asks to work with it
  2. Otherwise, create a new tab with mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_create_mcp
  3. If a tool returns an error indicating the tab doesn't exist or is invalid,
  call tabs_context_mcp to get fresh tab IDs
  4. When a tab is closed by the user or a navigation error occurs, call
  tabs_context_mcp to see what tabs are available</browser_instruction>

❯ about the qualitative report, you say "No one ever states a next screening" -
  I think a reminder for "the next screening" is not yet implemented and this
  is probably the idea of this task to suggest such a feature.

  Do you think that is a plausible speculation from my side?

  Listed 1 directory (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Yes, that's plausible, and it's more than a guess. The data pack says it
  outright. But "a reminder" on its own probably isn't the whole answer the case
  is steering towards.

  Why your reading holds
  - The journey file states it directly: "The report has no recommendation for
    when to come back for the next screening." [JOURNEY] Nothing in the journey
    contacts the customer after the report.
  - The booking form offers "I'd like reminders and offers", and the Berlin
    customer ticked it: "Has received nothing since the report." [INT-02] We
    collect consent for reminders and then never send any.
  - The ophthalmologist's evaluation form has no field for an interval, and she
    says she would fill one in. [INT-04]
  - An optician asks for exactly this: "Eine automatische Erinnerung an den
    Kunden, aber mit unserem Logo." [An automatic reminder to the customer, but
    with our logo.] [SURVEY-1]

  Why "build a reminder" alone falls short
  The constraints look designed to block the naive version:
  1. Who sets the date. A reminder needs a date, and an interval for a specific
     customer is a medical statement. It must come from the ophthalmologist's
     evaluation, not be set by Mirantus. A flat "remind everyone after 12
     months" breaks that rule. [CONSTRAINTS] So the interval has to start in the
     ophthalmologist's form and reach the customer through the report.
  2. Who can receive it. Reminders need marketing consent. A next-screening date
     in the report reaches everyone, because the report is a service message. A
     reminder months later reaches only the 54.2% with consent. [CSV]
  3. Who sends it. The optician owns the relationship, so a reminder has to go
     out on the optician's behalf with their name on it, or opticians will block
     it ("Bitte keine Mails von Mirantus an meine Kunden" [Please no e-mails
     from Mirantus to my customers]). [SURVEY-4]

  So the likely shape is a chain: the doctor records an interval, the report
  shows it, and a reminder in the optician's name goes to customers with
  consent. Which of those links a squad builds first is the real decision.

  Two cautions
  - Don't anchor on it. CLAUDE.md says the analysis decides where the gap is. A
    strong alternative framing is the German-only report: non-German speakers
    open it far less (37.8% vs 71.8%). [CSV] Step 3 should let the reminder idea
    compete with that and with an optician "due list", not assume it wins.
  - The data can't size it. The CSV has no interval column, so we can't show
    that non-returners are late rather than not yet due. The memo will have to
    admit that, and the 2-week test should cover that gap.

  Shall I go on to step 3 (insights and framings) with this in mind?

❯ yes, move on. For the later parts, where we are suggesting a feature: keep in
  mind to discuss how we as mirantus can send mails on behalf of the optician.
  Can we use their logos? Can we send from their e-mail addresses? If yes, how
  would the technical setup and the process look like. Don't do anything about
  this now, but keep it in your memory for later

  Read 1 file, ran 4 shell commands, wrote 1 memory (ctrl+o to expand)

  I read /Users/simondeichsel/gits/mirantus-case-2nd-run/data/constraints.md

  ## BLOCKING

  1. **Insight 6 (heading and "For the decision") and Framing A: the consent
  rule is stated wrongly.** The draft calls the report and the "result ready"
  e-mail "the only legal place to ask for reminder consent again". The rule
  says: "Consent can be asked for again later, but only in a message the
  customer already receives legitimately, **or in person**." So asking in person
  at the optician is also legal, and so are the other service messages. This
  matters for the framing choice: the counter is a consent channel the opticians
  control.
     **Fix:** quote the rule in full, add "in person" as a channel, and drop
  "only".

  2. **Insight 6 heading and body: "The report reaches every customer legally" /
  "the only touchpoints that reach everyone".** Legally the report can go to
  everyone, but only 65.6% (n=5905) open it. The heading mixes up legal reach
  with actual reach. "A next-screening date there is the channel for the 45.8%
  without consent" overstates it: 16.0% (n=1441) have neither consent nor an
  opened report.
     **Fix:** separate legal reach from actual reach in the heading. Say that a
  date in the report reaches non-consenting customers only if they open it.

  3. **Framing A ("Would build") clashes with the Medical advice rule.** Framing
  A puts the doctor's recommended next screening in the "result ready" e-mail.
  The rule says: "Anything that sounds like a diagnosis or a treatment
  recommendation may only come from the evaluating ophthalmologist, **in the
  report**." It also says the interval "must come from the ophthalmologist's
  evaluation". Today that e-mail "contains no result" [JOURNEY]. A personal
  interval in the e-mail body is a medical statement outside the report. The
  framing says it was checked against Medical advice but does not address this.
     **Fix:** keep the interval in the report only. The e-mail can say the
  report contains the doctor's recommendation for the next screening, without
  the interval itself. If you keep it in the e-mail, argue explicitly why that
  passes the rule.

  4. **Insight 5 and Framing B "Leaves out": "Half of store customers ... 50.2%
  (n=4515)".** In the analysis table (section 6), 50.2% is a share of **all**
  2025 customers (n=9000), not of store customers (n=8540). The share of store
  customers is not in the analysis output, and CLAUDE.md does not allow
  computing it in prose.
     **Fix:** write "50.2% of all 2025 customers (n=4515) are store customers
  whose optician reports no follow-up". Or extend `scripts/analyze.py` to output
  the share of store customers and cite that.

  5. **Insight 1 (Quantitative): the censoring rule is broken.** "By month 15,
  87.8% of all observed returners have returned" comes from the all-returners
  histogram (n=304). The analysis itself says that histogram "under-counts late
  returns". Late-2025 customers can only show up as early returners, so this
  inflates the share before 15 months. The Q1-only histogram (n=105 returners,
  fully observed to month 18) is in the analysis and has a heavier tail (14-15:
  24.8%; 15-16: 9.5%). The "10-15 months" cluster claim and Contradiction 2
  depend on this. It also matters for the window choice: a 15-month window would
  miss a visible share of Q1 returns.
     **Fix:** extend the script to output the Q1-cohort cumulative share by
  month, or use the Kaplan-Meier curve, and state n.

  6. **Insight 3: a contradiction between sources is smoothed over.** The
  doctor's line "It's the 'unremarkable' 60-year-olds who disappear" [INT-04] is
  placed as support. The data shows the opposite: 60+ with an unremarkable
  result return at 4.9% (n=878), above the cohort average of 4.7% and well above
  18-39 at 1.8% (n=560) [CSV]. CLAUDE.md says: "Name contradictions between
  sources; never smooth them away." A possible way to reconcile them: a 15-month
  window cannot judge the doctor's 1-2-year interval for 40+ [INT-04]. That
  point needs stating.
     **Fix:** add this as a named contradiction, with the interval caveat.

  ## SHOULD

  7. **Insight 3: medians cited without their basis.** "Median 384 vs 372 days"
  is among all returners by export, across all cohorts and censored, not the
  primary cohort. It also leaves out the fact that minor findings return at
  nearly three times the rate (9.4%, n=742), which is the stronger hint at
  shorter intervals.
     **Fix:** label the basis and soften "odd".

  8. **Insight 3: inference presented as source.** "Nobody asked, so no shorter
  interval could be set" joins INT-01 (nobody asked) with INT-04 (risk factors
  are not asked, so she "often can't tell who should come back sooner").
     **Fix:** cite both. This also shows a gap in Framing A: an interval field
  without any risk-factor question gives the doctor little to personalise on.
  Add it to Framing A's main risk or as an open question.

  9. **Contradiction 4: quote truncated so its meaning shifts.** The full INT-03
  quote is "Older customers find it pushy **if you call them for something they
  didn't ask for**." The qualifier partly resolves the contradiction: customers
  who gave consent did ask.
     **Fix:** quote it in full and adjust the contradiction.

  10. **Contradiction 2: misattributed quote.** "jährlich" [yearly] is tagged
  [SURVEY-8] [SURVEY-10], but SURVEY-10 says "Jahres-Check" [annual check-up] (a
  bundled offer), not "jährlich".
      **Fix:** quote each answer separately.

  11. **Framing A: capacity assumption not tagged.** The capacity rule names
  only the report PDF and e-mails as changeable. Whether the ophthalmologist's
  evaluation form can be changed this quarter is not stated anywhere.
      **Fix:** tag it [ASSUMPTION] and add it to the open questions.

  12. **Framing A: inconsistent with Framing C on translation.** Framing C says
  translating medical assessments needs doctor sign-off [Medical advice], but
  Framing A also translates the doctor's recommendation and does not mention
  this.
      **Fix:** state that the interval is a structured value shown through
  pre-approved templates in each language, or apply the same caveat to A.

  13. **Framing A: optician authorisation left open.** The rule says Mirantus
  may contact customers "on the optician's behalf and with the optician's name
  on it". That implies the optician authorises it. Opticians in SURVEY-4 and
  INT-03 reject Mirantus mail. Framing A does not say whether opticians opt in
  or opt out, or what happens for opticians who decline.
      **Fix:** make optician opt-in explicit (an offered tool, no extra work)
  and say what non-participating stores get.

  14. **Channel evidence missing.** SURVEY-14 says "SMS statt E-Mail. Unsere
  Kunden lesen keine E-Mails." [SMS instead of e-mail. Our customers don't read
  e-mails.] INT-01 reads e-mail "ab und zu" [now and then]. INT-02 says "Just
  WhatsApp me". Only e-mail can be changed this quarter [Capacity]. So an e-mail
  reminder may not land.
      **Fix:** add this as a contradiction or open question and as a risk on
  Framing A.

  15. **Insight 5: supporting evidence missing.** SURVEY-11 ("Der Befund sollte
  von uns kommen" [The report should come from us]; customers no longer remember
  they were at our store) directly supports sending in the optician's name.
  SURVEY-13 (opticians earn little from screening; it only pays when glasses are
  sold afterwards) bears on whether opticians want returns at all.
      **Fix:** cite both.

  16. **Insight 1 confidence: overclaims agreement.** "Three independent sources
  agree" does not hold. SURVEY-6 is a hypothetical, and the CSV only lacks a
  column, which does not show the journey lacks an interval. JOURNEY and INT-04
  are the real evidence.
      **Fix:** reword.

  17. **Insight 2: consent ownership unclear.** Consent is collected on "the
  optician's website or our booking page" [JOURNEY]. Whose consent it is (the
  optician's or Mirantus's) decides who may send the reminder.
      **Fix:** add it as an open question.

  18. **Framing A main risk: untagged claim.** "First intervals fall due 12+
  months out" needs a tag. It rests on [INT-04], whose shortest interval is
  yearly.

  Sign-off: no. Findings 1-6 have to be fixed before CP1. Three of them (1-3)
  misstate the Consent and Medical advice rules in exactly the parts that make
  Framing A the recommended one.

  04-decision-memo.md (outputs/04-decision-memo.md). It passes the checks at the
  600-word limit. The memo has one line on sending in the optician's name. I'll

› Message from reviewer
  Review of
  /Users/simondeichsel/gits/mirantus-case-2nd-run/outputs/04-decision-memo.md

  **Checker:** `python3 scripts/check_outputs.py` reports "OK: all checks
  passed". The memo is exactly 600 words by the checker's own count, which is
  the hard limit. Any fix below that adds words needs an equal cut. `--stale`
  reports step 4 as "never recorded". `data/constraints.md` has not changed
  since commit 2e5de33, so the memo correctly has no changed-rule line.

  ## BLOCKING

  1. **2-week test, "Customers" bullet: the research call breaks "The optician
  owns the relationship" and needs optician work.**
     - The rule: "Mirantus may contact customers only for the service itself, or
  on the optician's behalf and with the optician's name on it." A research call
  is not the service. Agreeing in person to a call does not lift the contract
  rule. `marketing_consent` does not cover it either.
     - "Those agreeing in person" means the examiner asks at the counter. The
  examiner is usually optician staff [JOURNEY]. That is unplanned optician work
  ("Opticians can't be required to do extra work").
     - The comparison is also broken. Customers at the 8 comparison locations
  got no interval, so asking them "when should you come back?" has no right
  answer and measures nothing.
     - Fix: run it only at stores whose optician opts into the test. Use a
  written research-consent slip handed out at the counter, which is optional for
  staff. Make the call "im Auftrag von [Optiker]" [on behalf of [optician]]. Or
  drop the call and put a one-question recall item on the report download page,
  which is part of the service. Compare recall before and after the change at
  the same stores, not against stores without an interval.

  2. **Success metric: the cohort, target and baseline do not describe the same
  population, and the definition is incomplete.**
     - The cohort is "first screenings with an interval recorded". The target is
  "8.0% for 12-month-interval customers". The baseline is 4.7% (n=3511) for
  *all* customers in a calendar window.
     - Under INT-04 guidance, 12-month intervals go to 40+ and risk customers.
  Those segments already return above 4.7%: 60+ at 6.2% (n=1264), minor findings
  at 9.4% (n=742) [CSV]. So 8.0% could be hit by composition alone, with no
  effect from the product.
     - The definition leaves these open:
       - whether a return before the interval counts;
       - whether a return at any mira location counts, or only at the same one;
       - how "per referral practice" customers are handled (no numeric interval:
  excluded?);
       - when the first readout is. The earliest is launch + 15 months, and
  36-month customers only at +39 months.
     - Fix: state the cohort per interval value, with the target for the
  12-month group only. Add a concurrent control, for example a random reminder
  holdout among consenting customers. Open question 7 in 03-insights already
  says a holdout is needed. Use the control, not the 2025 calendar rate, as the
  baseline. If a proxy is kept, restrict it to the segments likely to get 12
  months, and say it is a proxy for that reason. Spell out the excluded groups
  and the first readout date.

  3. **Target plausibility rests on the wrong channel and on a confounded
  association (Success metric, "Target").**
     - The memo justifies 8.0% with 11.7% "with consent and phone follow-up
  (n=273)" [CSV]. The memo presents that as support, not as an association. Per
  03-insights it is self-reported and rests on six independent stores and one
  chain store.
     - The memo recommends *e-mail*. The comparable cell in 01-data-analysis is
  optician e-mail follow-up: 4.6% (n=1120) vs none 4.0% (n=1939). Among
  independent stores with consent, e-mail follow-up is 3.6% (n=361) vs 5.0%
  (n=501) with no follow-up.
     - This is the strongest evidence against the e-mail reminder, and the memo
  leaves it out.
     - Fix: say "associated with". Name the e-mail follow-up result as a risk,
  and say why a doctor-dated reminder in the optician's name differs from a
  generic optician e-mail. Pick a target that does not lean on the phone cell,
  or mark it as low confidence.

  ## SHOULD

  4. **What ships first, item 3 ("points to the recommendation"):**
     - Framing A in 03-insights says the result-ready e-mail mentions the
  recommendation "without stating it". The memo is ambiguous. Putting the
  interval in the e-mail body would put a medical statement outside the report.
  The rule: "may only come from the evaluating ophthalmologist, in the report".
     - The due-date reminder (item 4) needs the same care. It should say the
  doctor's recommendation is in the report, not restate it as advice.
     - Fix: write "says the report contains the doctor's next-screening
  recommendation, without stating it".

  5. **Item 2 and the doctor test: "Ihre nächste Vorsorge" / "Nächste Vorsorge
  in X Monaten" [your next preventive check-up].**
     - "Vorsorge" reads as a medical preventive exam. The rule: "mira is a
  non-medical screening. It is not a replacement for an eye examination."
     - Fix: use "Ihr nächstes Screening" [your next screening] (as in SURVEY-6),
  next to the existing non-replacement note.

  6. **Non-German customer (items 2–4):**
     - Only the report block is said to be in the preferred language. Non-German
  speakers open the report at 37.8% (n=1633) [CSV], so the block misses most of
  them.
     - 03-insights Insight 4 concludes that "the result e-mail and that part of
  the report" must speak their language. The memo does not say this for the
  result-ready e-mail or the reminder.
     - The 2-week test uses the German-only existing report, so it never tests a
  non-German customer.
     - Fix: state that items 3 and 4 are sent in the preferred language through
  templates. Add one non-German check to the test, or name the gap.

  7. **Why this, bullet 2:**
     - "opticians ask for ('mit unserem Logo')" [with our logo] [SURVEY-1]
  stretches 1 of 15 answers into what opticians in general want.
     - It leaves out the objections: SURVEY-4 "Bitte keine Mails von Mirantus an
  meine Kunden" [please no Mirantus e-mails to my customers] and INT-03.
     - It also leaves out the customer contradiction: INT-02, "But not from the
  optician. I don't even remember which store it was."
     - Fix: say "one optician asks for…" and name the contradiction in one
  clause.

  8. **Why this, bullet 3, and the cut list (due-list):**
     - "needs staff time [SURVEY-9]" ignores SURVEY-3 and INT-03, who ask for
  exactly that list.
     - A due-list is allowed as an optional tool ("can be offered tools").
     - The reason that holds is that it needs a doctor's interval that does not
  exist yet. Lead with that.

  9. **Cut list, other reasons:**
     - Mobile units are cut for "weaker evidence". They have the lowest rate in
  the data: 1.5% (n=199, n=3 returns) [CSV]. The real reasons are small n and no
  clear owner whose name the reminder could carry (03-insights, Open question
  6).
     - Risk-factor questions are also cut for "weaker evidence". INT-04 and
  INT-01 both point to them, and without them intervals may become age defaults.
  The honest reason is capacity and booking-flow scope; name the
  default-interval risk.

  10. **Leading indicator vs. decision rule:** the indicator target is "interval
  recorded on 90% of evaluations", while the test continues at "80% of
  reports". Use one number, or explain the gap.

  11. **2-week test, feasibility and decision rule:**
      - Routing evaluations from 8 locations to 3 specific ophthalmologists is
  an untagged assumption. Tag it [ASSUMPTION] and confirm it with ops.
      - The expected number of reports in two weeks is not given. With about 30
  screenings a month across 2 stores [INT-03], it will be small; the memo must
  not type a computed number, but should flag the small sample as directional.
      - "Most called customers recall" and "most opticians opt in" have no
  thresholds or n.
      - A stated willingness to opt in after seeing a mockup is intent, not
  behaviour.
      - The rule has no "change" branch for customers who fail to recall.
      - Fix: set numeric thresholds with [TARGET] and add the change branch.

  12. **The test does not check the [ASSUMPTION] that the evaluation form can be
  changed** (item 1). The Capacity rule names only "The report PDF and e-mails"
  as changeable, and CP1 lists this as a "would change if" condition.
      - Fix: confirm with engineering before CP2, or name a fallback, such as a
  structured line in the free-text assessment that is parsed into the report.

  13. **Guardrails:**
      - "65.6% (n=5905)": 5905 is the number of opened reports; the denominator
  is n=9000.
      - "Reminder unsubscribes and optician opt-outs, weekly" has no threshold.
      - The same n problem appears in "54.2% … (n=4880)" in Why this, bullet 2;
  the denominator is n=9000.
      - Fix: give denominators and an alert threshold [TARGET].

  14. **Item 4 (reply-to the optician, opted-in stores):**
      - Replies landing in the optician's inbox can create staff work, so say
  replies need no answer or go to a booking link.
      - At chains HQ decides [SURVEY-7], so opt-in for chains is at HQ level,
  not per store. Say so.

  15. **Length:** 600 words is at the hard limit and well over the ≈500 target.
  Cut wording from "Why this" and "Cut, and why" to make room for the fixes
  above.

  Sign-off: no. The memo cannot pass CP2 until the research-call compliance
  problem (1), the mismatched metric cohort, baseline and target (2), and the
  unaddressed e-mail follow-up evidence (3) are fixed.

› Message from reviewer
  Second review of
  /Users/simondeichsel/gits/mirantus-case-2nd-run/outputs/04-decision-memo.md

  `python3 scripts/check_outputs.py`: "OK: all checks passed". The memo is
  exactly 600 words (`wc -w`), so it is at the hard limit and any added word
  breaks it. Every fix below swaps words rather than adding them.

  **Prior BLOCKING issues**
  1. **Research call vs. "optician owns the relationship": resolved, with one
  leftover (see SHOULD 1).** The call now happens only at opted-in stores, only
  for customers who sign an optional slip, and "on the optician's behalf". That
  fits the rule "on the optician's behalf and with the optician's name on it"
  and "can't be required to do extra work".
  2. **Cohort, baseline and target mismatch: mostly resolved.** The definition
  (interval + 3 months), the cohort (numeric interval, reported per interval)
  and the proxy baseline (15-month window, n=3511) now line up. The target is a
  reminded-vs-holdout difference. What remains is in SHOULD 2 and 3.
  3. **Target plausibility: resolved.** The target now cites e-mail follow-up at
  4.6% (n=1120) vs. 4.0% (n=1939), worded as "associated with" and marked low
  confidence. Both numbers are in `01-data-analysis.md`.

  **BLOCKING**
  - **B1. "2-week test", decision rule: "6 of 10 opticians opt in" has no tag.**
  It is a threshold we set, so the evidence rule says it must be tagged
  [TARGET]. To stay at 600 words, add the tag and drop a word elsewhere, e.g.
  "incl." in the Opticians line or "(more than week 1)".

  **SHOULD**
  1. **Test setup: the memo doesn't say who hands out the research slip, or who
  calls and under which name.** If store staff hand it out, that is optician
  work. It is voluntary at opted-in stores, but say so. Fix: "slip in the
  result-ready e-mail; Mirantus calls in the optician's name".
  2. **Baseline doesn't match the target population.** The +2pp target applies
  to consenting customers in the 12-month group. The baseline shown is all
  customers plus age bands. The analysis already has consent=true at 5.7%
  (n=1975). Fix: replace the two age-band figures with that number, which saves
  words.
  3. **Readout timing and power.** At "launch + 15 months" only customers
  screened in the launch month have finished their window. A 20% holdout drawn
  from consenting customers at opted-in stores in the 12-month group will be
  small, so +2pp on about 5% probably can't be detected at that point. Fix: tie
  the readout to a minimum holdout size, or call the first readout directional.
  4. **Test outcome measure is weak.** In week 1 there is no interval to name,
  so "more than week 1" is trivially met. Fix: measure "names the interval the
  doctor gave".
  5. **Test timing.** Week-2 customers screened late in the week only get the
  report up to 48 hours later [JOURNEY], so some calls fall outside the two
  weeks. Fix: screen in days 8–12 and call by day 14.
  6. **Customers who don't read German.** The test's assessment line is German
  text in a German report, and the memo says "Non-German templates untested".
  Non-German customers open the report at 37.8% vs. 71.8% for German
  (`01-data-analysis.md`), so the report block reaches them least. Fix: call
  this out as a known gap in the trade-off, or have the doctor add the line in
  the customer's preferred language for the test.
  7. **Contradiction not named.** INT-02 says "But not from the optician… I'd
  want it from whoever has my results". The memo sends reminders in the
  optician's name. A short clause in Why this bullet 2 would cover it.
  8. **Cut list: "intervals may default by age" has no tag and touches the
  Medical advice rule.** The rule says an interval for a specific customer "must
  come from the ophthalmologist's evaluation, not from marketing copy". A
  default set by age reads like the system choosing it. Fix: "doctor sets
  interval; age guidance [INT-04]", or drop the clause. "SMS/WhatsApp: capacity"
  also has no tag; add [CONSTRAINTS], since that file only lists the report PDF
  and e-mails as changeable.
  9. **The holdout only measures the reminder.** The report block also reaches
  customers without consent, and nothing measures its effect except the leading
  indicator (interval filled on 90% of evaluations). This is acceptable but
  worth one word in the Trade-off.
  10. **"incl. objectors [SURVEY-4]".** The survey answers aren't attributed to
  anyone, so we can't pick out the objectors. Write "incl. objectors like
  [SURVEY-4]" or recruit through a screening question.

⏺ You're right. The call measured what customers say, not what they do, and it
  put Mirantus on the phone with the optician's customers. I replaced the test
  with a live, lightweight first version. 04-decision-memo.md
  (outputs/04-decision-memo.md) is at 599 words and passes the checks.

  The new test: a live first version at 4 stores that opt in
  - What changes for customers:
    - The ophthalmologist picks 12, 24 or 36 months in a small new form field.
    - Page 1 of the report shows "Ihr nächstes Screening" [your next screening]
      with an "In Kalender eintragen" [add to calendar] button.
    - The "result ready" e-mail, in the optician's name, offers "Erinnerung per
      E-Mail" [e-mail reminder].
  - What we measure for 2 weeks:
    - Calendar adds and reminder opt-ins per opened report, including customers
      without prior consent.
    - How often the doctors fill in the interval.
    - Optician complaints.
  - Decision rule: roll out if doctors fill in 90% [TARGET], 20% of customers
    who open the report add the date or opt in [TARGET], and no store drops out.
  - What ships after the test: the reminder itself, sent when the interval is
    due. It can't fire within two weeks, so a reminder opt-in is our best signal
    of demand, and it also collects consent.

  Your question: can we settle the medical-statement issue before building?
  Yes. It is a question of rules and wording, not something to test on users.
  Before launch, the evaluating doctors and Mirantus legal approve the
  templates. My reading of [CONSTRAINTS], marked as an [ASSUMPTION] in the memo
  for legal to confirm:
  - What the rule blocks: an interval that Mirantus or the optician chooses,
    such as "come back every year".
  - What it allows: the doctor's own interval, recorded in their evaluation.
  - How the design stays inside the rule:
    - The date appears only in the report.
    - The customer creates the calendar entry themselves, from the report.
    - The e-mail and the later reminder only say "your report contains your
      doctor's recommendation" and link to it. They never state the interval
      themselves.

  Two consequences:
  1. This is no longer a test "on the existing product", which CLAUDE.md asks
     for. It needs a short build first: the form field, the report block, the
     calendar file and the e-mail button. I'll log that as your decision at CP2.
  2. The reminder only goes out when the interval is due, so the success
     metric's first readout is still launch + 15 months. The 2-week test shows
     uptake, not returns.

⏺ I've drafted 05-prototype-spec.md (outputs/05-prototype-spec.md) and it passes
  the checks. It contains:
  - The flow screen by screen: doctor form, optician opt-in card, result e-mail
    with and without consent, report block, double opt-in, reminder at the due
    date, booking and unsubscribe.
  - Every message in full German copy, each with the constraint sections it was
    checked against.
  - A Turkish version of the result e-mail and the report block.
  - §4 on sending in the optician's name, as you asked:
    - The logo is used under a licence the optician grants when opting in.
    - For v0, mails go out from a Mirantus subdomain with the optician's name as
      the sender name. Mirantus handles SPF/DKIM/DMARC, so the optician has no
      work.
    - Sending from the optician's own domain becomes a later opt-in that needs
      DNS records on their side.
    - Sending through the optician's own systems is not possible this quarter.
    - Mirantus sends as a data processor under a GDPR agreement.
    - It also describes the optician's opt-in process step by step.
  - A ready-to-paste prompt for Claude Design.

  Review of
  /Users/simondeichsel/gits/mirantus-case-2nd-run/outputs/05-prototype-spec.md

  Checker: `python3 scripts/check_outputs.py` returned "OK: all checks passed
  for the outputs that exist." No failures. `data/constraints.md` is unchanged,
  so the spec is right to say no rule changed.

  ## BLOCKING

  1. **The 20% holdout breaks a promise the copy makes (§3 M1-A, S3, S4, M1-B,
  M3 conditions).**
     - M1-A tells consenting customers "schicken wir Ihnen eine E-Mail, wenn es
  laut Befund so weit ist".
     - M1-B, S3 and S4 tell opted-in customers they will get "genau eine E-Mail"
  [exactly one e-mail].
     - M3 then withholds that e-mail from 20% of them on purpose. That makes the
  copy untrue for one in five recipients. For Customer B, who explicitly asked
  for the reminder through double opt-in, it is a consent and trust problem.
     - Fix: randomise before the promise is made. Either the holdout's M1 has no
  reminder sentence and no offer, or randomise by store. State which, and check
  the metric's baseline still holds.

  2. **Referral customers are promised a reminder that can never be sent (M1-A,
  M1-B, S1, Turkish M1-B).**
     - When D1 = "Gemäß Facharztpraxis", S2 has no date and M3 cannot fire.
     - Yet M1-A says "wir schicken Ihnen eine E-Mail, wenn es laut Befund so
  weit ist".
     - M1-B and S1 offer "Ja, erinnern Sie mich" and say page 1 holds "die
  Empfehlung … für Ihr nächstes Screening".
     - Fix: add an M1 referral variant that drops the reminder sentence and the
  offer, and points to the referral block. Add it to the flow table and the
  Claude Design prompt.

  3. **Optician name used without the optician's agreement, and an undefined
  state for stores that have not opted in (§1, §3 "All messages", O1, §6).**
     - Every message carries the optician's name, logo and "im Auftrag von
  {Optiker}". But only O1 opt-in grants name and logo use, and only
  "deactivating stops reminders" is defined.
     - For stores that have not opted in, and for mobile units (which are cut),
  the spec does not say what M1 looks like or whether it offers the reminder.
     - If it uses the store name, it puts the optician's name on mail they never
  agreed to. If it offers the reminder, it collects consent for a reminder
  "from {Optiker}" that will never be sent.
     - Rule: "Mirantus may contact customers only for the service itself, or on
  the optician's behalf and with the optician's name on it."
     - Fix: define M1 for non-opted-in stores and mobile units: Mirantus-branded
  service mail, no reminder offer, S2 block still shown.

  4. **The Claude Design prompt is not ready to paste (§7).**
     - It ends with "[Paste the full copy of D1, O1, S1–S6 … here.]" and says
  "German copy exactly as given below", but nothing follows.
     - Pasted as is, Claude Design will invent customer-facing copy that has not
  been checked against `data/constraints.md`. CLAUDE.md says "Never show
  customer-facing copy that has not been checked". The run-case skill requires a
  "ready-to-paste prompt".
     - Fix: inline the full checked copy (German and Turkish) in the code block.
     - Also align the footer in the prompt with §3. The prompt drops {Adresse}
  and applies the footer to "every … screen", which is wrong for screens.

  ## SHOULD

  5. **"Befund von {Optiker}" (S1, and "bei uns" in M1) attributes the medical
  report to the optician.**
     - Rule (Medical advice): "Anything that sounds like a diagnosis … may only
  come from the evaluating ophthalmologist".
     - Fix: "Ihr Befund zum Screening bei {Optiker} ist da".

  6. **"heutigen Aufnahmen" / "bugünkü görüntülerinizi" in S2 (DE and TR) is
  wrong.**
     - The report arrives up to 48 h later [JOURNEY] and is read later still.
     - Fix: "Ihrer Aufnahmen vom {Datum}" / "{Tarih} tarihli görüntülerinizi".

  7. **S2 hard-codes "Augenärztin".** Fix: add a gender variant, or use "{Dr.
  med. Name} (Augenheilkunde)".

  8. **The S2 line "Wenn Sie Beschwerden haben, wenden Sie sich bitte an eine
  Augenarztpraxis" is template text, not the doctor's.** It reads close to a
  treatment recommendation. Fix: list it explicitly among the template lines the
  week-0 doctor and legal review must approve.

  9. **"Reply goes to booking link" is not technically possible (O1, §4
  Replies).** Reply-To must be an e-mail address. Fix: Reply-To is a
  Mirantus-run address with an auto-reply that gives the booking link and store
  phone. Say who reads replies; if nobody does, say that too.

  10. **§4 technical details.**
      - Option 2: putting an SPF include on the optician's root domain risks the
  10-lookup limit or a clash with their existing SPF record. DMARC alignment
  only needs DKIM d=optician domain, or a custom Return-Path subdomain (CNAME).
  Fix: "DKIM CNAME(s) + Return-Path CNAME", no root SPF edit.
      - "Never … would fail DMARC" only holds if the optician publishes
  p=quarantine/reject. With no DMARC record or p=none, the mail passes
  unauthenticated. Reword it as an authenticity and deliverability rule.
      - Add RFC 8058 one-click List-Unsubscribe headers to M1-A and M3
  (bulk-sender requirements at Gmail and Yahoo).

  11. **The GDPR roles are oversimplified (§4 Legal set-up).**
      - Mirantus runs the screening and contracts the doctors, so it is very
  likely controller for the screening data, not only the optician's processor.
      - The recommended interval is derived from health data (Art. 9). Using it
  to time a reminder likely needs explicit consent covering that use. The
  booking checkbox "reminders and offers" may not cover it.
      - Fix: mark controller/processor/joint controllership (Art. 26) and the
  Art. 9 basis as open legal questions next to Open question 9. Make the M2
  confirmation text name the use of the report's recommended date.

  12. **The flow breaks for non-German readers after M1-B (§5).** A Turkish
  customer who taps "Evet" lands on S3, M2, S4 and S6, which are only specified
  in German, and S1 is German. Fix: state that S1, S3, M2, S4, S6 and M3 render
  in the preferred language, using the same templates.

  13. **v0 promises reminders before M3 exists (§6, M1-A).** The memo's decision
  rule includes "Doctors won't fill: stop". Customers in the 4 test stores have
  then been promised a reminder. Fix: commit to sending M3 to everyone opted in
  during the test, or soften the wording in the v0 copy.

  14. **v0 build scope versus two weeks (§6).**
      - v0 needs D1, the PDF block, M1 A/B, S3, M2, S4, S6 and an O1 dashboard
  card.
      - For 4 stores, O1 can be a manual setup by Mirantus. Fix: say so, and
  move the O1 build after the test.

  15. **Evidence tags.**
      - "Card on the existing optician dashboard" (O1) is untagged. The only
  hint in the sources is [SURVEY-3] "Liste im Dashboard". Tag it
  [SURVEY-3]/[ASSUMPTION].
      - "many customers are 60+" in the §7 prompt is untagged. Either tag [CSV]
  using the analysis's 60+ n=3187 of n=9000, or drop it.
      - [INT-03] "pushy" is about calling older customers about something they
  didn't ask for. It only loosely supports "no follow-up chaser" on a reminder
  they opted into. Say "by analogy".

  16. **Missing per-copy constraint lines.** The §3 footer, S5, and the D1
  tooltip-level copy have no checked-against line. The skill requires one under
  each piece of copy. Add them.

  17. **The optician-facing O1 copy overstates the sender.** "Absender ist Ihr
  Geschäft" is not accurate under Option 1, where the From address is on the
  Mirantus domain. Fix: "Absendername ist Ihr Geschäft".

  18. **The reading of the Consent rule is not stated.** Bullet 1 says reminders
  need consent "at booking". The spec relies on bullet 3 ("Consent can be asked
  for again later…") to treat consent given in M1-B as valid. Fix: state that
  reading explicitly in the M1-B constraint line.

  19. **Turkish copy.** No outright errors; three fixes:
      - "Merhaba {Ad} {Soyad}" clashes with the formal "Saygılarımızla". Use
  "Sayın {Ad} {Soyad},".
      - The S2 line "**12 ay** sonra, yani **{Ay Yıl}** itibarıyla önerilir."
  has no subject and reads awkwardly. Use "Önerilen zaman: **12 ay** sonra, yani
  **{Ay Yıl}** itibarıyla."
      - §5 should cite the Turkish-specific figure, 37.6% (n=474) [CSV], which
  is in `outputs/01-data-analysis.md`, alongside the non-German aggregate.

  Sign-off: not as is. Findings 1–4 (holdout promise, referral variant, M1 at
  non-opted-in stores, incomplete Claude Design prompt) must be fixed first.

          -ician uploads it in O1 and grants                             
      341 -a usage licence limited to these messages in the opt-in terms.
          - Chains: HQ uploads once for all                              
      342 -stores [SURVEY-7]. Without a logo, the optician's name in text
          - is enough to meet the rule.

          +hains: HQ uploads once for all

          - <erinnerung@versand.mirantus.example>` | Mirantus only: SPF,

          -arter.** No work for the optician. |

          -erinnerung@optik-muster.example>` | Optician adds 2–3 DNS reco
          -rds (DKIM CNAME, SPF include) that Mirantus provides; Mirantus
          - verifies them | Later, opt-in for opticians who want it, main
          -ly chain HQs with IT. Small independents often have no one to

          +and this quarter.** No work for the optician. |

⏺ I revised the spec and it passes the checks. Changes from the first review:
  - Holdout: customers are drawn into the holdout before any reminder is
    mentioned, and holdout customers get the Mirantus-branded result e-mail
    (M1-N), so none of them is promised a reminder.
  - New variants: M1-R for referral results, with no reminder. M1-N for stores
    that haven't opted in, mobile units and the holdout, branded mira.
  - Claude Design: you paste the whole file. The prompt allows only the checked
    copy, word for word.
  - Smaller fixes:
    - Replies go to a Mirantus address with an auto-answer.

› Message from reviewer
  Review round 2:
  /Users/simondeichsel/gits/mirantus-case-2nd-run/outputs/05-prototype-spec.md

  I read data/constraints.md fresh, and it is unchanged. `python3
  scripts/check_outputs.py` reports "OK: all checks passed", so there are no
  checker failures. Every number in §5 is in outputs/01-data-analysis.md: 37.8%
  (n=1633), 71.8% (n=7367) and 37.6% tr (n=474), at lines 438–444. The quotes
  [SURVEY-3], [SURVEY-7] and [INT-03] are verbatim or marked as translations.

  **The four BLOCKING issues from round 1**
  1. Holdout vs. reminder promises: **resolved.** The holdout is drawn before
  any reminder is mentioned and gets M1-N. M3 excludes holdout customers. There
  is no holdout in the 2-week test.
  2. Referral customers promised a reminder: **resolved.** M1-R and the S2
  referral variant contain no reminder, and M3 excludes referrals.
  3. M1 for stores that have not opted in and for mobile units: **mostly
  resolved.** M1-N exists and the optician's name only appears after opt-in,
  except for the new BLOCKING issue B1 below.
  4. Claude Design prompt not ready to paste: **resolved.** "Paste whole file"
  plus "use only the copy in §2, §3, §5" works. See S3 for a smaller
  inconsistency.

  **BLOCKING**

  - **B1. §1 "Which M1 a customer gets", rule order (lines 46–47), together with
  §3 "Sender and footer" (line 215).** Rule 1 sends every referral result to
  M1-R before rule 2 checks whether the store opted in. So a referral customer
  at a store that has not opted in, or at a mobile unit, gets M1-R: "From:
  {Optiker}", signed "Ihr Team von {Optiker}" [your team at {optician}], with
  the on-behalf footer. That uses the optician's name without opt-in, and a
  mobile unit has no optician name at all. It breaks the Relationship rule:
  "Mirantus may contact customers only for the service itself, or on the
  optician's behalf and with the optician's name on it." It also contradicts §3
  ("M1-R … go only to customers of opted-in stores") and the M1-N note ("For
  referral results the third paragraph is the M1-R wording"). **Fix:** make
  "store not opted in or mobile unit → M1-N (with the referral paragraph for
  referral results)" rule 1, and referral → M1-R rule 2. Add a "Geschäft nicht
  freigeschaltet + Überweisung" [store not activated + referral] path to the
  prompt, or at least say in §1 that it exists.

  **SHOULD**

  - **S1. §1 rule 3 and M1-N: the holdout gets a different sender as well as no
  reminder.** Holdout customers at opted-in stores get the Mirantus-branded
  M1-N, while the treated group gets the optician-branded M1-A/M1-B with the
  reminder offer. The memo's metric compares "reminded vs holdout". As
  specified, that comparison mixes up three things: the sender branding, the
  reminder offer and the reminder itself. **Fix:** give the holdout at opted-in
  stores an optician-branded M1 with no reminder sentence and no offer (an
  M1-A/M1-B body without that paragraph). Or state explicitly that the metric
  measures the whole package.
  - **S2. M3 eligibility (lines 392–394): no suppression for customers who
  already came back.** A customer who rebooks before the due date, which the
  metric counts as "early counts", still gets "Dieser Zeitpunkt ist jetzt
  erreicht" [that time has now come]. Their old interval is then also superseded
  by the new report's interval. **Fix:** add "no screening recorded since the
  report" to the M3 conditions, and say that a new report replaces the due date.
  - **S3. §1 "Language toggle" (line 59) vs. §5 and §7 (line 592).** §1 promises
  DE/TR on M1-B, S1, S2, S3, M2, S4 and M3. Turkish copy exists only for M1-B
  and S2, and the prompt only toggles those two. Because the prompt says to use
  only given copy and leave grey boxes where copy is missing, a fuller toggle
  would show grey boxes. **Fix:** cut the §1 list down to M1-B and S2 for the
  prototype, and state that the other templates are translated before launch.
  - **S4. M1-A (lines 243–244 and 259–263): the copy promises the reminder while
  the legal basis is open.** "Da Sie bei der Buchung Erinnerungen zugestimmt
  haben, schicken wir Ihnen eine E-Mail …" [as you agreed to reminders when
  booking, we'll send you an e-mail] goes live in the 2-week test (§6).
  Meanwhile §4 says it is open whether the booking checkbox covers using the
  report's date. If it does not, this promise is wrong for real customers.
  **Fix:** make this a week-0 legal decision, alongside the template approvals.
  Specify the fallback M1-A copy now: the M1-B offer plus M2, worded for
  customers who already gave consent.
  - **S5. S2 referral variant (line 155): the template line is not on the
  approval list.** "Bitte besprechen Sie den Zeitpunkt mit der Augenarztpraxis,
  an die Sie überwiesen wurden" [please agree the timing with the ophthalmology
  practice you were referred to] is a care instruction written as template text,
  not by the doctor. Unlike the "Wenn Sie Beschwerden haben …" [if you have
  symptoms …] line, it is not flagged for week-0 approval. Medical advice rule:
  "Anything that sounds like a diagnosis or a treatment recommendation may only
  come from the evaluating ophthalmologist, in the report." **Fix:** add it to
  the week-0 approval list in the S2 notes.
  - **S6. S2 for mobile units (line 141): "Ihr Screening-Ort: {Optiker},
  {Adresse}" [your screening location: {optician}, {address}] has no defined
  value** for a mobile unit. **Fix:** say what is shown, for example the mobile
  unit's location on the screening date, and that no return place is implied.
  - **S7. Memo vs. spec, S2 branding.** Memo item 2 says the report block
  carries the "optician's name and logo". The spec's S2 names the optician only
  as the screening location and has no logo. **Fix:** align the two. Logo only
  for opted-in stores fits the Relationship rule; say so in the S2 notes.

  Sign-off: not as is. B1 has to be fixed first (a one-line change to the rule
  order); after that I would sign off, with S1 and S2 strongly recommended.

⏺ The second round found one more BLOCKING finding: the order of the routing
  rules lets a store that hasn't opted in send referral e-mails in the
  optician's name. I'll fix that and the 7 SHOULD findings.

      48 -- **Decision rule:** roll out if doctors fill 90% [TARGET], 20%
         - of recipients opt in [TARGET], 8 of 15 opticians opt in [TARGE
         -T], and no store drops out. Fewer opt-ins: rethink the sender b
         -efore building more. Low uptake: rework placement and wording, 
         -rerun. Doctors won't fill: stop.                               
      47 +- **Decision rule:** roll out if doctors fill 90% [TARGET], 20%
         + of recipients opt in [TARGET], 8 of 15 opticians opt in [TARGE
         +T], and no store drops out. Few opticians: rethink the sender.

      473 -| Option | From line the customer sees | Setup | Use |        
      474 -|---|---|---|---|                                             
      475 -| 1. Mirantus sending subdomain, optician as display name | `O
          -ptik Muster <erinnerung@versand.mirantus.example>` | Mirantus 
          -only: SPF, DKIM and DMARC on its own sending subdomain | **v0 
          -and this quarter.** No work for the optician. |               
      476 -| 2. Optician's own domain, sent by Mirantus | `Optik Muster <
          -erinnerung@optik-muster.example>` | Optician adds DNS records 
          -that Mirantus provides: DKIM CNAME(s), so DKIM signs with the 
          -optician's domain (enough for DMARC alignment), plus a Return-
          -Path CNAME on a subdomain. No change to the optician's root SP
          -F record, which avoids SPF lookup limits and clashes with exis
          -ting records. | Later, opt-in for opticians who want it, mainl
          -y chain HQs with IT. Small independents often have no one to e
          -dit DNS, so it can't be the default. |                        
      477 -| 3. Sent from the optician's own mail system or CRM | — | Int
          -egration with the optician's systems | Not possible this quart
          -er [CONSTRAINTS, Capacity]. |

          +elationship; Capacity.

      479 -Rule: Mirantus never puts an optician's own address in "From" 
          -without option 2's DNS set-up.                                
      480 -Depending on the optician's DMARC policy, such mail is rejecte
          -d, lands in spam, or arrives                                  
      481 -unauthenticated; in every case it presents a sender that has n
          -ot authorised the mail.                                       
      482 -                                                              
      483 -**Replies.** Reply-To is a Mirantus-run address per store (e.g
          -.                                                             
      484 -`antwort+{store}@versand.mirantus.example`). The optician pick
          -s in O1:                                                      
      485 -- *Default:* an automatic answer with the booking link and the
          - store's phone number; nobody at                              
      486 -  Mirantus or the store reads replies, and the auto-answer say
          -s so. No staff work.                                          
      487 -- *Option:* replies are forwarded to the store's own e-mail ad
          -dress.                                                        
      488 -                                                              
      489 -**Legal set-up: open questions for Mirantus legal, before M3 g
          -oes live.**                                                   
      490 -- Roles: Mirantus runs the screening and contracts the doctors
          -, so it is likely a controller of                             
      491 -  the screening data itself, not only the optician's processor
          -. Sending reminders in the                                    
      492 -  optician's name may make the two joint controllers (Art. 26 
          -GDPR) or keep Mirantus as processor                           
      493 -  for the mailing (Art. 28). To decide.                       
      494 -- Health data: the interval is derived from health data (Art. 
          -9 GDPR). Using it to time a                                   
      495 -  reminder likely needs explicit consent covering that use. M2
          - and S3 therefore name the use of                             
      496 -  the report's date. Whether the booking checkbox "reminders a
          -nd offers" [JOURNEY] covers it for                            
      497 -  M1-A customers is open; if not, M1-A customers also get the 
          -M2 confirmation step.                                         
      498 -- Who holds the booking consent (`03-insights.md`, Open questi
          -on 9).                                                        
      499 -                                                              
      500 -**Process for an optician:**                                  
      501 -1. Mirantus partner management offers it (v0: in person for th
          -e 4 test stores; later the O1                                 
      502 -   card). Chains: HQ decides [SURVEY-7].                      
      503 -2. The optician gives the sender name and logo, picks the repl
          -y option, previews M1 and M3.                                 
      504 -3. The optician accepts the opt-in terms (logo licence, data-p
          -rotection agreement).                                         
      505 -4. Mirantus checks the logo and name once and activates.      
      506 -5. From then on, the store's customers get M1-A/B/R and M3 in 
          -its name. Deactivation at any time                            
      507 -   switches back to M1-N; S2 stays.

      513 -Customers whose preferred language is not German open the repo
          -rt far less often: 37.8%                                      
      514 -(n=1633) vs 71.8% (n=7367) [CSV]. For Turkish (`tr`) the open 
          -rate is 37.6% (n=474) [CSV]. All                              
      515 -customer screens and messages render in the preferred language
          - from the same templates. The                                 
      516 -Turkish copy needs a native-speaker check before launch [ASSUM
          -PTION]; the German originals are                              
      517 -M1-B and S2.                                                  
      316 +- **Week 0:** optician opt-ins (of 15 offered); legal and doct
          +or sign-off on S2, M1, M3.                                    
      317 +- **Weeks 1–2, at 4 opted-in stores:** share of evaluations wi
          +th an interval (D1); M1-B sent,                               
      318 +  opened, "Ja, erinnern Sie mich" tapped, M2 confirmed, by age
          + band; unsubscribes; optician                                 
      319 +  complaints or drop-outs.                                    
      320 +- M3 is not needed in the two weeks: it fires 12+ months later
          +. It is built after the test.

  are recorded again, and all steps are up to date. I didn't rerun the reviewer
  on the narrowed spec. The copy is mostly the version it already reviewed, but
  I can run it if you want.

  What changed
  - Optician acceptance comes first. 05-prototype-spec.md
    (outputs/05-prototype-spec.md) §1 sets out the split evidence: SURVEY-1 and
    SURVEY-9 for, SURVEY-4 and INT-03 against, SURVEY-7 says head office
    decides.
    - Before anything is built for customers, partner managers offer the
      reminder to 15 opticians, including stores like the objectors and at least
      one chain HQ. They show each optician exactly what their customers would
      receive.
    - The gate is 8 signed opt-ins out of 15 [TARGET]. Below that, we rethink
      who sends the reminder before building more. The memo now says the same.
  - Technical setup is now simple. Mail goes out from a Mirantus domain with the
    optician's name as sender, so the optician configures nothing. The logo is
    optional, and replies get an automatic answer. Sending from opticians' own
    domains is dropped.
  - German only, reminder mail only. I removed the Turkish copy, the holdout and
    referral variants, the variant for stores that haven't opted in, and the

    - The flow is the result e-mail (with or without consent), the opt-in
      confirmation, and the reminder mail.
